Subject:** Analytic number theory — Riemann Hypothesis, zero-free regions, zeta function\n\n**Statement:** The Riemann zeta function has no zeros in the region Re(s) > 7/8, strengthening the classical de la Vallée-Poussin zero-free region. This improves the best known zero-free strip approaching the critical line.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Pushing the zero-free boundary from Re(s) > 1 to Re(s) > 7/8 brings the exclusion zone closer to the critical line Re(s) = 1/2. The classical zero-free region excluded Re(s) = 1. Hadamard and de la Vallée-Poussin showed no zeros on Re(s) = 1. The Vinogradov-Korobov region gives no zeros for Re(s) >= 1 - c/log|t| for some constant c. This result establishes a uniform strip Re(s) > 7/8 free of zeros — a constant-width improvement. The key technique involves refined estimates for exponential sums and the Montgomery-Vaughan approach to zero-density. If extended to Re(s) > 1/2, this would imply the Riemann Hypothesis. The gap from 7/8 to 1/2 remains.\n\n**Significance:** Best known zero-free region for the Riemann zeta function, a step toward the Riemann Hypothesis.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists (lean/docs/001.md).\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 001.*
Subject:** Additive number theory — primes, arithmetic progressions, Szemerédi regularity\n\n**Statement:** The primes contain arithmetic progressions of arbitrary length. For every positive integer k, there exist a and d such that a, a+d, a+2d, ..., a+(k-1)d are all prime.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Pseudorandomness + density transfer. The primes have density zero in the integers, so Szemerédi's theorem does not directly apply. Green and Tao embed the primes into a pseudorandom measure (the W-tricked primes) and prove a transference principle: any subset of positive relative density of a pseudorandom set contains long APs. The key ingredients are (1) the Gowers uniformity norm U^{k-1} controlling AP counts, (2) a relative Szemerédi theorem for dense subsets of pseudorandom sets, and (3) the Goldston-Yıldırım type sieve showing the W-tricked primes are sufficiently pseudorandom. The pattern: transfer from dense sets to sparse sets via a pseudorandom host.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves a long-standing conjecture — primes contain arbitrarily long arithmetic progressions.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists (lean/docs/002.md).\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 002.*
Subject:** Extremal graph theory — graph regularity, quasirandomness, approximation\n\n**Statement:** For every epsilon > 0 and integer m, there exists M such that every graph on sufficiently many vertices admits an epsilon-regular partition into at most M parts, with all but epsilon fraction of pairs being epsilon-regular.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Every large graph is approximately a union of quasirandom bipartite graphs. The regularity lemma decomposes any graph into a bounded number of parts where the edge densities between parts are approximately constant. The pattern: approximation by structure — a bounded-complexity weighted graph (the reduced graph) captures the large-scale behavior. The key: the energy increment argument. If a partition is not regular, refining it increases the energy (sum of squared densities), which is bounded above. This forces termination. The lemma underlies the Green-Tao theorem, property testing, and graph removal lemmas.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational structure theorem for large graphs; enables removal lemmas and additive combinatorics.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists (lean/docs/003.md).\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 003.*
Subject:** Extremal graph theory — graph removal, extremal removal, property testing\n\n**Statement:** If a graph G on n vertices contains o(n^3) triangles, then one can remove all triangles by deleting o(n^2) edges.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** If a graph is far from being triangle-free, it must contain many triangles. The contrapositive: if removing few edges suffices to make G triangle-free, then G already had few triangles. The proof uses the Szemerédi regularity lemma: partition G, identify irregular pairs and pairs with density above threshold, remove edges from dense pairs to destroy all triangles. The number of edges removed is o(n^2) because the regular partition has bounded parts. The pattern: the removal lemma connects local substructure counting to global editing distance. Generalizes to any fixed graph H.\n\n**Significance:** Connects extremal graph theory to property testing; implies Roth's theorem on 3-term APs.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists (lean/docs/004.md).\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 004.*
Subject:** Additive combinatorics — arithmetic progressions, density, ergodic theory\n\n**Statement:** Every subset of the integers with positive upper density contains arbitrarily long arithmetic progressions.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Positive density forces arithmetic structure. Szemerédi proved that for any k and delta > 0, any subset of {1,...,N} with at least delta*N elements contains a k-term AP for N large enough. The proof uses the regularity lemma. The pattern: density implies pattern — the denser the set, the richer the arithmetic structure. The theorem generalizes Roth (k=3, Fourier-analytic proof) and van der Waerden (density-free, coloring). Alternative proofs: Gowers gave a quantitative proof using higher-order Fourier analysis (uniformity norms), and Furstenberg gave an ergodic-theoretic proof using multiple recurrence. Each proof reveals a different structural facet.\n\n**Significance:** Central theorem of additive combinatorics; unifies density, regularity, and ergodic approaches.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists (lean/docs/005.md).\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 005.*
Subject:** Combinatorics — Ramsey theory, combinatorial lines\n\n**Statement:** For every positive integers n and k, there exists N such that any k-coloring of the n-dimensional grid [k]^N contains a monochromatic combinatorial line.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** High-dimensional grids force monochromatic structure under any coloring. A combinatorial line is a set of points where some coordinates are fixed and one coordinate varies freely. The Hales-Jewett theorem is the combinatorial backbone behind van der Waerden's theorem — density is not needed, only dimension. The pattern: dimension forces patterns even without density. The proof uses a double induction on the alphabet size k and dimension n. The key insight: combinatorial lines are the "right" notion of arithmetic progression in product spaces.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational result in Ramsey theory; implies van der Waerden's theorem and Graham's conjecture on Euclidean Ramsey theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 006.*
Subject:** Combinatorial geometry — incidence geometry, point-line arrangements\n\n**Statement:** The maximum number of incidences between n points and m lines in the plane is O(n^{2/3} m^{2/3} + n + m).\n\n**Meta-pattern:** The incidence count is governed by a single exponent 2/3 emerging from the crossing number inequality. The Szemerédi-Trotter bound is tight — achieved by a grid construction. The pattern: extremal incidence configurations have grid-like structure. The proof via the crossing number inequality gives a clean argument: if many incidences exist, the corresponding graph has few crossings, contradicting the crossing lemma. The key: the 2/3 exponent reflects a balance between point density and line density.\n\n**Significance:** Cornerstone of incidence geometry; implies Beck's theorem and bounds on unit distances.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 007.*
Subject:** Additive number theory — arithmetic progressions, Fourier analysis\n\n**Statement:** Any subset of {1,...,N} with density delta > 0 contains a 3-term arithmetic progression, provided N is sufficiently large as a function of delta.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Fourier-analytic density increment. Roth proved that any dense set of integers contains a 3-term AP. The proof uses the Fourier transform: the count of 3-APs is expressed as a sum involving the Fourier coefficients of the indicator function. If no 3-AP exists, the Fourier coefficients must be large, which forces a density increment on a subprogression. Iterating gives a contradiction for large enough N. The pattern: local Fourier uniformity controls arithmetic structure. The density bound is N >> delta^{-1} (improved to delta^{-1} (log 1/delta) by Bourgain, and further by Sanders).\n\n**Significance:** First instance of the Szemerédi theorem (k=3 case); pioneered Fourier-analytic methods in additive combinatorics.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 008.*
Subject:** Extremal combinatorics — corners, arithmetic progressions, graph removal\n\n**Statement:** Any subset of [N] x [N] with positive density contains a corner: three points of the form (x,y), (x+d,y), (x,y+d) with d > 0.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Two-dimensional density forces corner structure. The corners theorem is the 2D analogue of Roth's theorem. The proof reduces to the triangle removal lemma in a tripartite graph constructed from the dense set. The pattern: higher-dimensional density forces higher-dimensional arithmetic structure. The corners theorem is equivalent (up to polynomial factors) to the triangle removal lemma.\n\n**Significance:** Key ingredient in the proof of the triangle removal lemma; connects 2D combinatorics to graph theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 009.*
Subject:** Ramsey theory — arithmetic progressions, colorings\n\n**Statement:** For any positive integers k and r, there exists N = W(k,r) such that any r-coloring of {1,...,N} contains a monochromatic arithmetic progression of length k.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Colorings force monochromatic structure. Unlike Szemerédi's theorem (which needs density), van der Waerden needs only a finite coloring — any partition into finitely many classes forces an AP in one class. The proof uses double induction on k (progression length) and r (colors). The key construction: given W(k-1,r) and W(k,r-1), build a long interval where two blocks of length W(k-1,r) are placed so that if neither contains a k-AP, their color patterns force a (r-1)-coloring of a shorter interval, yielding W(k,r-1). The bounds are enormous — tower-type in r.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational result in Ramsey theory; motivated Szemerédi's theorem and the Green-Tao theorem.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 010.*
Subject:** Combinatorics — Ramsey theory, graph colorings\n\n**Statement:** For any integers k and r, there exists R(k,r) such that any r-coloring of the edges of the complete graph K_N with N >= R(k,r) contains a monochromatic K_k.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Complete disorder is impossible in large enough structures — any coloring of a sufficiently large complete graph forces a monochromatic subgraph. The proof uses induction on k and r: R(k,r) <= R(k-1,r) + R(k,r-1). Pick a vertex v; by pigeonhole, one color class from v contains at least R(k-1,r) or R(k,r-1) neighbors, yielding a monochromatic K_k by induction. The Ramsey numbers grow exponentially; exact values known only for small cases.\n\n**Significance:** Cornerstone of Ramsey theory; demonstrates that complete disorder is impossible in large combinatorial structures.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 011.*
Subject:** Combinatorics — counting, injectivity\n\n**Statement:** If n+1 objects are placed into n boxes, at least one box contains at least two objects. More generally, placing N objects into k boxes guarantees some box has at least ceil(N/k) objects.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** The most fundamental counting principle — finite partitions of large sets force collisions. Every Ramsey-type result is a sophisticated application of pigeonhole. The principle is equivalent to the statement that there is no injection from a set of size n+1 to a set of size n. The meta-pattern: averaging forces concentration — if the average is A, some element exceeds A. This underlies the Erdős averaging argument, the probabilistic method, and density increment strategies.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational principle underpinning virtually all of combinatorics and Ramsey theory.\n\n**Verification:** Trivial proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 012.*
Subject:** Combinatorics — sequences, Ramsey theory\n\n**Statement:** Any sequence of n^2+1 distinct real numbers contains a monotonically increasing or monotonically decreasing subsequence of length n+1.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Forcing monotonicity through length. The proof assigns each element its "rank pair" (a_i, b_i) where a_i is the length of the longest increasing subsequence ending at position i, and b_i is the longest decreasing. If all a_i <= n and all b_i <= n, there are at most n^2 distinct pairs, but n^2+1 elements force a collision by pigeonhole — impossible since distinct elements have distinct pairs. The pattern: dimension reduction via pair labeling. The bound n^2+1 is tight.\n\n**Significance:** Early application of the pigeonhole principle; motivated the development of Ramsey theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 013.*
Subject:** Order theory — partially ordered sets, chains, antichains\n\n**Statement:** In any finite partially ordered set, the maximum size of an antichain equals the minimum number of chains needed to cover the set.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Duality between chains and antichains. Dilworth's theorem is a min-max result: the largest antichain (pairwise incomparable set) has the same size as the smallest chain decomposition. The proof uses induction and König's theorem (bipartite matching). The dual result (Mirsky's theorem) states the maximum chain length equals the minimum antichain cover. The pattern: partition vs. packing duality, analogous to König-Egerváry in graph theory and LP duality.\n\n**Significance:** Central theorem of order theory; connects to matching theory and linear programming duality.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 014.*
Subject:** Combinatorial geometry — convex polygons, Ramsey theory\n\n**Statement:** For every n >= 3, there exists N such that any set of N points in general position in the plane contains n points forming a convex polygon.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Geometric forcing via Ramsey-type arguments. The proof constructs a monotone subsequence of slopes. The key insight: among enough points, either a convex n-gon exists or the point set has a special structure that forces a larger convex subset. The bound N = 2^{2n-4} + 1 was proved by Erdős-Szekeres. The pattern: geometric configurations are forced by combinatorial size, bridging geometry and Ramsey theory.\n\n**Significance:** Initiated the field of combinatorial geometry; led to the development of Ramsey theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Extremal graph theory — complete subgraphs, edge bounds\n\n**Statement:** The maximum number of edges in an n-vertex graph with no (r+1)-clique is (1 - 1/r) * n^2/2, achieved by the Turán graph T(n,r).\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Extremal structure is the balanced complete r-partite graph. Turán's theorem identifies the exact extremal graph: the balanced complete r-partite graph maximizes edges while avoiding K_{r+1}. The pattern: the densest K_{r+1}-free graph is the most uniform multipartite graph. The proof uses induction or the weight-shifting argument of Zykov symmetrization. The theorem extends to hypergraphs via the Erdős-Stone theorem.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational result in extremal graph theory; determines the Turán number for complete graphs.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Combinatorics — matrix theory, design theory\n\n**Statement:** A Hadamard matrix of order n exists only if n = 1, 2, or n ≡ 0 (mod 4). The entries are ±1 with pairwise orthogonal rows, satisfying H * H^T = nI.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Orthogonality under sign constraints. The Hadamard conjecture posits that the divisibility condition n ≡ 0 (mod 4) is also sufficient. Hadamard matrices maximize determinant among ±1 matrices: |det H| = n^{n/2}. The pattern: combinatorial designs achieving extremal algebraic properties. Constructions use Sylvester (powers of 2), Paley (prime constructions via quadratic residues), and tensor product methods.\n\n**Significance:** Central object in coding theory, combinatorial design, and signal processing.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Analysis/Algebra — inner product spaces, inequalities\n\n**Statement:** For any vectors u, v in an inner product space, |⟨u,v⟩|^2 ≤ ⟨u,u⟩⟨v,v⟩, with equality iff u and v are linearly dependent.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** The fundamental inequality relating inner products to norms. The proof: expand ⟨u - λv, u - λv⟩ ≥ 0 as a quadratic in λ; its non-negativity forces the discriminant ≤ 0. The pattern: positivity of a quadratic form forces an inequality between coefficients. Cauchy-Schwarz underlies the triangle inequality, Bessel's inequality, and Hölder's inequality. It is the algebraic backbone of Hilbert space theory.\n\n**Significance:** Perhaps the most widely used inequality in mathematics; foundational for functional analysis.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 018.*
Subject:** Analysis — convexity, expectations, inequalities\n\n**Statement:** If f is convex and X is a random variable, then f(E[X]) ≤ E[f(X)]. For concave f, the inequality reverses.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Convex functions and expectations commute in one direction. The proof follows from the supporting hyperplane characterization: f(x) ≥ f(a) + f'(a)(x - a), taking expectations gives E[f(X)] ≥ f(E[X]). The pattern: convexity transforms linear averaging into an inequality. Jensen's inequality underlies the AM-GM inequality, the power mean inequality, and information-theoretic inequalities (e.g., Gibbs' inequality via the concavity of entropy).\n\n**Significance:** Central inequality in probability, optimization, and information theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 019.*
Subject:** Analysis — Lp spaces, inequalities\n\n**Statement:** For p,q > 1 with 1/p + 1/q = 1, ∫|fg| ≤ (∫|f|^p)^{1/p} (∫|g|^q)^{1/q}. Equivalently, ||fg||_1 ≤ ||f||_p ||g||_q.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Conjugate exponents control product integrals. The proof uses Young's inequality: ab ≤ a^p/p + b^q/q, applied to normalized functions. The pattern: conjugacy of exponents creates a duality between Lp spaces. Hölder generalizes Cauchy-Schwarz (p=q=2) and underlies the duality of Lp spaces. Iterated Hölder gives the generalized inequality for multiple functions.\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental inequality in analysis; defines the dual structure of Lp spaces.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Analysis — Lp spaces, norms, inequalities\n\n**Statement:** For p ≥ 1, ||f+g||_p ≤ ||f||_p + ||g||_p. The Lp norm satisfies the triangle inequality.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** The triangle inequality for Lp norms. The proof uses Hölder's inequality: expand ∫|f+g|^p = ∫|f+g|^{p-1}|f+g|, split and apply Hölder to each term. The pattern: convexity of t^p for p ≥ 1 enables the norm property. Minkowski is the geometric fact that Lp is a normed space for p ≥ 1.\n\n**Significance:** Establishes that Lp spaces are normed vector spaces; foundational for functional analysis.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Algebra/Analysis — inequalities, means\n\n**Statement:** For nonnegative real numbers a_1,...,a_n, the arithmetic mean is at least the geometric mean: (a_1+...+a_n)/n ≥ (a_1·...·a_n)^{1/n}, with equality iff all a_i are equal.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Averaging and multiplication are dual through convexity. The inequality follows from Jensen's inequality applied to the convex function f(x) = -log(x), or from the concavity of the geometric mean. The pattern: the arithmetic mean (linear) dominates the geometric mean (multiplicative) because log is concave. AM-GM is the simplest case of the power mean inequality and connects to entropy via the concavity of log.\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental inequality; used across algebra, analysis, optimization, and information theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Geometry — plane curves, optimization\n\n**Statement:** Among all plane figures with given perimeter, the circle encloses the maximum area. Equivalently, A ≤ L^2/(4π) for area A and perimeter L.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Symmetry optimizes geometric functionals. The proof uses the Brunn-Minkowski inequality or the Wirtinger inequality. The pattern: the most symmetric shape extremizes the ratio of area to perimeter. Generalizes to higher dimensions (sphere optimizes volume/surface area) and to discrete settings (Harper's theorem on the hypercube).\n\n**Significance:** Foundational geometric inequality; connects to PDE, spectral theory, and combinatorics.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Convex geometry — Minkowski sums, volumes\n\n**Statement:** For compact convex sets A,B in R^n, vol(A+B)^{1/n} ≥ vol(A)^{1/n} + vol(B)^{1/n}, where A+B is the Minkowski sum.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Volume of Minkowski sums is superadditive in the 1/n-th root. The proof for convex bodies uses the Prékopa-Leindler inequality (a functional form). The pattern: the n-th root of volume is concave under Minkowski addition — convexity of sets translates to concavity of the volume functional. This is the geometric analogue of the AM-GM inequality and implies the classical isoperimetric inequality.\n\n**Significance:** Central inequality in convex geometry; implies isoperimetric inequality and has functional analogues.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. The proof first establishes the result on L1∩L2 using the Fourier inversion formula, then extends by density. The pattern: the Fourier transform diagonalizes convolution and translation, and the L2-isometry reflects the symmetry of the frequency-domain representation. Plancherel underlies the spectral theory of translation-invariant operators.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational theorem of harmonic analysis; establishes Fourier transform as a unitary operator.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Harmonic analysis — Fourier transform, localization\n\n**Statement:** If |f(x)| ≤ C e^{-π x^2} and |f̂(ξ)| ≤ C e^{-π ξ^2}, then f is a constant multiple of e^{-π x^2}. A function and its Fourier transform cannot both be too concentrated.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Localization in time and frequency are in tension. The proof for the Gaussian case uses complex analysis (Phragmén-Lindelöf or direct calculation). The pattern: the Fourier transform exchanges spatial decay and frequency decay; only Gaussians achieve the optimal simultaneous decay. This is the harmonic-analytic form of the quantum uncertainty principle Δx·Δp ≥ ℏ/2.\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental limit on simultaneous localization; connects harmonic analysis to quantum mechanics.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Harmonic analysis — Fourier series, theta functions\n\n**Statement:** For suitable f, Σ_{n∈Z} f(n) = Σ_{k∈Z} f̂(k). The sum of f over integers equals the sum of its Fourier transform over integers.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Periodization in one domain corresponds to sampling in the dual. The proof: the periodized sum F(x) = Σ f(x+n) has Fourier coefficients f̂(k), so F(0) = Σ f̂(k). The pattern: summing a function on a lattice equals summing its transform on the dual lattice. This connects the discrete and continuous and is fundamental to theta function identities and modular forms.\n\n**Significance:** Bridges Fourier analysis, number theory, and physics; essential for theta functions and modular forms.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. The pattern: band-limited functions are exactly entire functions of controlled exponential growth. This characterizes the Fourier transform as a bijection between Paley-Wiener spaces. The theorem connects the support of the Fourier transform to the analytic continuation properties of f.\n\n**Significance:** Characterizes band-limited functions; foundational for signal processing and sampling theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Complex analysis — approximation by rational functions\n\n**Statement:** Let K be a compact subset of C and f holomorphic on a neighborhood of K. Then f can be uniformly approximated on K by rational functions whose poles lie in the complement of K.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Holomorphic functions on compact sets can be approximated by rational functions with prescribed singularity structure. The proof uses Cauchy's integral formula: approximate the Cauchy integral by Riemann sums, which are rational functions. If the complement of K is connected, the rational functions can be replaced by polynomials. The pattern: approximation theory in complex analysis is governed by the topology of the complement — connectivity allows polynomial approximation.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational approximation theorem in complex analysis; generalizes to the Mittag-Leffler theorem.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Complex analysis — conformal mappings\n\n**Statement:** If U is a simply connected proper open subset of C, then there exists a biholomorphic map from U onto the open unit disk D.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Every simply connected domain is conformally equivalent to the disk. The proof uses a normal families argument: construct a family of injective holomorphic maps into the disk, show it is normal (Montel's theorem), and extract a map maximizing the derivative at a point. The maximizer is surjective by a contradiction argument using square roots. The pattern: extremal problems in function spaces combined with compactness yield existence of conformal maps.\n\n**Significance:** Central theorem of complex analysis; establishes universality of the disk as the model simply connected domain.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Complex analysis — zeros, analytic continuation\n\n**Statement:** If f is holomorphic on a connected open set U and the zeros of f have a limit point in U, then f is identically zero on U.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Zeros of holomorphic functions are isolated unless the function vanishes identically. The proof uses the power series expansion: if f(z_0) = 0 and f is not identically zero, then f(z) = (z - z_0)^m g(z) with g(z_0) ≠ 0, so z_0 is an isolated zero. A limit point of zeros forces all Taylor coefficients to vanish. The pattern: analytic rigidity — local information (a limit point of zeros) determines global behavior (f ≡ 0).\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental rigidity theorem; underpins analytic continuation and the uniqueness of holomorphic extensions.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 031.*
Subject:** Complex analysis — holomorphic functions, boundary behavior\n\n**Statement:** If f is holomorphic on a connected open set U and |f| attains a local maximum at a point in U, then f is constant. Equivalently, |f| attains its maximum on the boundary of compact subsets.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Holomorphic functions have no interior maxima of modulus. The proof uses the mean value property: f(z_0) equals the average of f on a circle around z_0, so |f(z_0)| is bounded by the maximum on the circle. Equality forces f to be constant on the circle by equality in the triangle inequality. The pattern: analytic functions satisfy a mean value property that prevents interior extrema, analogous to harmonic functions.\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental theorem of complex analysis; implies the Schwarz lemma and underlies potential theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 032.*
Subject:** Complex analysis — holomorphic self-maps of the disk\n\n**Statement:** If f: D → D is holomorphic with f(0) = 0, then |f(z)| ≤ |z| for all z in D and |f'(0)| ≤ 1. If equality holds for any nonzero z or at 0, then f is a rotation: f(z) = e^{iθ} z.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Holomorphic self-maps of the disk fixing the origin are contractions. The proof: g(z) = f(z)/z extends holomorphically to 0 (removable singularity) and maps D to D̄, so by the maximum modulus principle |g(z)| ≤ 1. The pattern: rigidity of holomorphic maps — the disk is a model space with a rich automorphism group, and maps fixing the origin are controlled by the Schwarz lemma. Generalizes to the Schwarz-Pick lemma for non-fixed-point maps via automorphisms.\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental rigidity theorem; cornerstone of geometric function theory and hyperbolic geometry.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 033.*
Subject:** Functional analysis — extension of linear functionals\n\n**Statement:** Let X be a real vector space, p a sublinear functional on X, and f a linear functional on a subspace M such that f(x) ≤ p(x) for all x in M. Then f extends to a linear functional F on all of X with F(x) ≤ p(x).\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Dominated linear functionals extend from subspaces to the whole space. The proof uses Zorn's lemma: consider the partially ordered set of all extensions of f satisfying the domination condition, show every chain has an upper bound, and extract a maximal extension that must be defined on all of X. The pattern: local linear constraints propagate globally via order-theoretic maximality. Hahn-Banach separates convex sets, underlies duality theory, and is the analytic foundation of the "extension by domination" principle.\n\n**Significance:** Cornerstone of functional analysis; enables separation theorems, duality, and the existence of enough continuous linear functionals.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 034.*
Subject:** Functional analysis — bounded operators, surjectivity\n\n**Statement:** If T: X → Y is a bounded surjective linear operator between Banach spaces, then T is an open map — it maps open sets to open sets.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Surjective bounded linear maps between Banach spaces are automatically open. The proof uses the Baire category theorem: X = ∪ T^{-1}(nB_Y), so some T^{-1}(B_Y) has nonempty interior, which by linearity forces T to map a ball around 0 to a set containing a ball around 0. The pattern: Baire's theorem (completeness) forces quantitative openness from qualitative surjectivity. This is one of the three fundamental principles of linear functional analysis (with Hahn-Banach and closed graph).\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental theorem of functional analysis; implies the bounded inverse theorem and the closed graph theorem.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Functional analysis — closed operators, Banach spaces\n\n**Statement:** If T: X → Y is a linear operator between Banach spaces and the graph of T is closed in X × Y, then T is bounded.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** A closed graph forces boundedness in Banach spaces. The proof: the graph G(T) is a closed subspace of the Banach space X × Y, hence itself a Banach space. The projection π1: G(T) → X is a continuous bijection, so by the bounded inverse theorem T = π2 ∘ π1^{-1} is continuous. The pattern: closedness of the graph (a qualitative condition) implies quantitative continuity, via Baire category. One of the three fundamental principles of linear functional analysis.\n\n**Significance:** Central theorem of functional analysis; provides a practical criterion for boundedness.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Functional analysis — Hilbert spaces, dual spaces\n\n**Statement:** For every bounded linear functional f on a Hilbert space H, there exists a unique y in H such that f(x) = ⟨x, y⟩ for all x in H, and ||f|| = ||y||.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Hilbert spaces are self-dual — every functional is an inner product. The proof: if f ≠ 0, take y0 in ker(f)⊥ with f(y0) = 1, then y = y0/||y0||² satisfies the representation. Uniqueness follows from the nondegeneracy of the inner product. The pattern: the inner product provides a canonical isomorphism between H and its dual H*, identifying functionals with vectors. This self-duality is the defining feature distinguishing Hilbert spaces from Banach spaces.\n\n**Significance:** Cornerstone of Hilbert space theory; underlies weak convergence, adjoint operators, and spectral theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Algebra — field extensions, Galois groups\n\n**Statement:** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension with Galois group G = Gal(L/K). There is a bijection between intermediate fields K ⊆ F ⊆ L and subgroups H ≤ G, given by F ↦ Gal(L/F) and H ↦ L^H. The bijection reverses inclusions and [L:F] = |H|, [F:K] = [G:H].\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Intermediate fields correspond to subgroups of the Galois group, reversing the lattice order. The proof: for a Galois extension, L/L^H is Galois with group H (Artin's theorem), and L^Gal(L/F) = F (since L/K is normal and separable). The pattern: the automorphism group of an extension encodes its entire intermediate field structure. The correspondence reverses inclusions: larger subgroups fix smaller fields. Normal subgroups correspond to Galois intermediate extensions.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational theorem of algebra; establishes the dictionary between field theory and group theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Lean formalization exists.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 070.*

Pattern: the thin-shell phenomenon — random points on a convex body concentrate near a sphere of fixed radius — forces the isotropic constant to be dimension-free, analogous to how concentration of measure controls the variance of linear functionals. The extension to cylinder coverings shows the plank bound is intrinsic to the convex body's width geometry, not just Euclidean strips. The sharp lower bound confirms that planks are optimal coverers.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves Bang's conjecture on plank/cylinder coverings — a classical problem in convex geometry generalizing Tarski's plank problem.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Algorithms / computational biology — trace reconstruction, deletion channel, sample complexity, uniform decoders\n\n**Statement:** At every fixed deletion probability in (0,1), reconstructing an arbitrary length-n binary string requires n^{Omega(log log n)} independent traces, ruling out polynomial-sample reconstruction. A uniform decoder achieves quasipolynomial sample and running-time bounds for known fixed rational retention probabilities. When the deletion probability is at most n^{-epsilon} for fixed epsilon > 0, both bounds become polynomial in the input and parameter encoding.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Superpolynomial sample complexity barrier with polynomial rescue at low deletion rates. Trace reconstruction asks: given multiple noisy copies (traces) of a binary string passed through a deletion channel, recover the original. Pattern: the n^{Omega(log log n)} lower bound rules out polynomial samples — quasipolynomial is necessary. The uniform decoder (same algorithm for all strings) achieves matching quasipolynomial bounds. The phase transition at deletion probability n^{-epsilon} is key: when deletions are rare (subpolynomial), polynomial samples suffice; when deletions are constant-rate, quasipolynomial is both necessary and sufficient. This reflects the information-theoretic bottleneck: constant-rate deletion destroys most positional information, requiring superpolynomial samples to statistically distinguish strings.\n\n**Significance:** Establishes tight quantitative bounds for trace reconstruction — resolving the sample complexity of the deletion channel reconstruction problem.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. The proof uses separation of asymptotic expansions on nested complex domains and a finite-dimensional counting argument.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Uniform cycle bounds via complex analytic separation and finite-dimensional counting. Hilbert's 16th problem (second part) asks for the maximum number of limit cycles of a polynomial vector field of degree n on the plane. Pattern: the existence of a finite bound H(n) depending only on degree was the conjecture — this proves it. The exact value H(5)=2 for quintic Liénard systems (ẋ=y-F(x), ẏ=-x, deg F≤5) settles the Lins Neto–de Melo–Pugh conjecture. The technique of separating asymptotic expansions on nested complex domains is analytic: the limit cycles' behavior is controlled by studying the complexified vector field on nested annuli in C^2, where asymptotic expansions can be separated by domain nesting. The finite-dimensional counting argument then bounds the number of zeros of a finite set of functions derived from these expansions. The proof has two companion papers: one for the general uniform bound, one for the sharp Liénard quintic case.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves the uniform boundedness part of Hilbert's 16th problem — one of the most famous open problems in dynamical systems and differential equations.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Ergodic theory / smooth dynamics — Banach's spectrum problem, Koopman operator, Lebesgue spectrum, volume-preserving diffeomorphisms\n\n**Statement:** Resolves the probability-preserving form of Banach's simple Lebesgue-spectrum problem within smooth dynamics. The construction on T^3 with C^∞ smoothness and volume preservation is remarkable — it shows smooth dynamics can realize the spectral extremity Banach sought. The Koopman operator linearizes the dynamics: studying its spectral properties reveals the mixing structure of the transformation. Lebesgue spectrum is the "maximally mixing" spectral type — it implies mixing of all orders. The three-torus is the minimal dimension for such constructions in the smooth volume-preserving category.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves Banach's simple Lebesgue-spectrum problem in the smooth probability-preserving setting — a landmark in spectral ergodic theory open since the 1930s.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Ergodic theory — mixing, multiple mixing, probability-preserving transformations, correlations\n\n**Statement:** Every invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation is mixing of all finite orders. Correlations among any finite collection of measurable sets converge to the product of their measures whenever all pairwise time separations diverge.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Pairwise mixing implies all-order mixing for a single transformation. Rokhlin asked whether mixing (2-fold) implies mixing of all orders (k-fold for every k). Pattern: mixing of order k means μ(A₀ ∩ T^{-n₁}A₁ ∩ ... ∩ T^{-n_k}A_k) → ∏ μ(A_i) when all time separations n_{i+1}-n_i → ∞. The result shows 2-fold mixing suffices — no need for separate k-fold mixing hypotheses. This is the strongest possible implication: the weakest mixing notion implies the strongest. The "all pairwise time separations diverge" condition is the natural generalization of n → ∞ for higher-order correlations.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves Rokhlin's multiple-mixing problem — a foundational question in ergodic theory open since the 1940s.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Dynamical systems / ergodic theory — standard map, metric entropy, Sinai conjecture, area-preserving maps\n\n**Statement:** The standard sine map on the two-dimensional torus has positive metric entropy with respect to area for every sufficiently large positive parameter. This establishes Sinai's positive-parameter-measure conjecture for the original family, with the stronger conclusion of a full parameter tail.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Uniform positive entropy for all large parameters via parameter-tail analysis. The standard map (Chirikov map) T_k(x,y) = (x + k sin(y), y + x + k sin(y)) mod 1 is a paradigmatic area-preserving map. The "full parameter tail" conclusion is stronger: not just positive measure of parameters, but ALL parameters beyond some threshold K have positive entropy. The standard map models kicked rotors and appears throughout Hamiltonian dynamics. For small k, KAM tori dominate; for large k, the phase space is predominantly chaotic. This result proves the transition is complete beyond a threshold.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves Sinai's conjecture for the standard map family — a central problem in Hamiltonian dynamics.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Dynamical systems — billiards, Birkhoff conjecture, convex caustics, integrable billiards\n\n**Statement:** A smooth strictly convex planar billiard with positive curvature is an ellipse whenever a full grazing annulus is continuously foliated by individually invariant essential curves. A continuous physical collar of smooth closed convex caustics also suffices.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Local integrability near the boundary forces global ellipticity. The Birkhoff conjecture states that the only integrable billiards (those with a foliation by invariant curves) are ellipses. Pattern: "near-boundary" means the foliation exists only in a collar region adjacent to the boundary (grazing annulus), not the entire phase space. The result shows even this local integrability condition forces the billiard to be an ellipse. Invariant essential curves correspond to caustics — curves tangent to all billiard trajectories. The grazing annulus is the phase-space region near the boundary where trajectories have near-tangential reflections. Continuous foliation by invariant curves is the integrability condition. The two sufficient conditions (grazing annulus foliation vs physical collar of caustics) are related but distinct formulations.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves the near-boundary Birkhoff conjecture — a major step toward the full Birkhoff conjecture on integrable billiards.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Subject:** Fractal geometry / probability — self-similar measures, Hausdorff dimension, entropy rate, random walks, contraction ratios\n\n**Statement:** For every self-similar measure on the line generated by finitely many contracting similarities, dim_H μ = min{1, h_RW/χ}, where h_RW is the entropy rate of random composed maps and χ the average logarithmic contraction. This resolves the entropy-rate dimension conjecture without a separation assumption, allowing exact overlaps and unequal contraction ratios.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Dimension as entropy-over-contraction ratio without separation conditions. Self-similar measures are generated by iterated function systems (IFS) of contracting similarities. Pattern: the formula dim_H = min(1, h_RW/χ) is the Moran/Hutchinson formula generalized to the overlapping case. h_RW is the entropy rate of the random walk on the IFS graph — it captures the effective randomness of which contraction is applied. χ is the average logarithmic contraction rate. Without separation (overlapping IFS), the dimension can drop below the naive value due to exact overlaps. The min with 1 reflects the ambient dimension bound. The key advance: NO separation assumption is needed — exact overlaps (where different composition paths yield the same point) and unequal contraction ratios are handled. The entropy rate h_RW naturally accounts for overlaps via the random walk structure.\n\n**Significance:** Resolves the entropy-rate dimension conjecture in full generality — without any separation condition, the central open problem in self-similar measure dimension theory.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Complex analysis — entire functions, product representations\n\n**Statement:** Every entire function f can be written as z^m e^{g(z)} \prod_{n=1}^{\infty} E_{p_n}(z/a_n) where a_n are the nonzero zeros of f, m is the order of zero at 0, g is entire, and E_{p_n} are elementary factors. Conversely, every such product defines an entire function with zeros at the prescribed points.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Entire functions are determined up to an exponential factor by their zeros. The proof constructs elementary factors E_p(z) = (1-z) exp(z + z^2/2 + ... + z^p/p) that approximate (1-z) while controlling convergence, then uses these to build a product converging to f/e^{g}. The pattern: the zero set of an entire function, together with an entire exponential factor, completely determines the function — analogous to the fundamental theorem of algebra for polynomials, but with convergence issues requiring the primary factors.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational theorem of complex analysis; generalizes the fundamental theorem of algebra to entire functions.\n\n**Verification:** Text proof. Subject:** Complex analysis — zeros of holomorphic functions, mean values\n\n**Statement:** If f is holomorphic on a neighborhood of |z| ≤ R, f(0) ≠ 0, and a_1,...,a_n are the zeros of f in |z| < R (counted with multiplicity), then log|f(0)| + \sum log(R/|a_k|) = (1/2π) \int_0^{2π} log|f(Re^{iθ})| dθ.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** The number and distribution of zeros inside a disk are controlled by the average of log|f| on the boundary. Subject:** Differential geometry / PDE — general relativity\n\n**Statement:** The Schoen-Yau positive mass theorem states that for an asymptotically flat 3-manifold (M, g) with nonnegative scalar curvature R >= 0, the ADM mass m_ADM satisfies m_ADM >= 0, with equality if and only if (M, g) is isometric to Euclidean R^3. The proof proceeds by contradiction: if the mass is negative, one constructs a stable minimal surface in the manifold and derives a contradiction using the stability inequality and the Gauss equation relating scalar curvature to the intrinsic and extrinsic curvature of the minimal surface. The Lohkamp extension handles the case of low regularity metrics.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Stable minimal surface stability inequality reveals the positive mass condition. Subject:** PDE / differential geometry — geometric analysis\n\n**Statement:** The Yamabe problem asks whether on a compact Riemannian manifold (M,g) of dimension n >= 3, there exists a conformal metric with constant scalar curvature. Yamabe (1960) claimed existence via minimizing the Yamabe functional Q(g) = (integral R_g dvol_g) / (vol_g)^{(n-2)/n} over conformal classes, but his proof had an error. Trudinger (1968) fixed the case where the Yamabe constant Y(M,[g]) is nonpositive. Aubin (1976) showed Y(M,[g]) <= Y(S^n,[g_0]) always holds and proved existence when the inequality is strict. Schoen (1984) completed the proof by showing strict inequality holds for all non-spherical manifolds using the positive mass theorem.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Conformal Laplacian and positive mass theorem reveal the Yamabe minimizer existence. The key step is showing that when Y(M,[g]) = Y(S^n), the manifold must be S^n with the round metric, via the stereographic projection and the positive mass theorem applied to the asymptotically flat conformal metric. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the scalar curvature alone does not reveal the conformal deformation; the Yamabe constant and positive mass obstruction provide the analysis.\n\n**Significance:** Resolution of a classical problem in geometric analysis; relevant to PDE and differential geometry.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. Subject:** Harmonic analysis — square functions\n\n**Statement:** The Littlewood-Paley square function S(f)(x) = (sum_{j in Z} |Delta_j f(x)|^2)^{1/2}, where Delta_j f = f * psi_j is the dyadic frequency projection with psi_j a smooth bump supported on an annulus {2^{j-1} <= |xi| <= 2^{j+1}}, satisfies the equivalence ||S(f)||_p ~ ||f||_p for 1 < p < infinity. The one-sided estimate ||f||_p <= C_p ||S(f)||_p for 1 < p <= 2 follows from Khintchine's inequality applied to the Rademacher functions, while the reverse ||S(f)||_p <= C_p ||f||_p for 2 <= p < infinity follows from duality. The sharp bound on the constants involves the L^p norm of the Rademacher projection, and the theory extends to martingale square functions and wavelet characterizations of Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Dyadic frequency decomposition and Khintchine randomization reveal the L^p equivalence. The proof uses the Calderon reproducing formula f = sum_j Delta_j f and the Khintchine inequality E_eps |sum eps_j Delta_j f|^p ~ (sum |Delta_j f|^2)^{p/2} for Rademacher variables eps_j, combined with the Marcinkiewicz multiplier theorem controlling the dyadic multipliers as L^p bounded operators. The key step is showing that the randomization by Rademacher functions decouples the dyadic pieces, converting the deterministic square function to a probabilistic moment estimate. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the function f alone does not reveal its frequency distribution; the dyadic decomposition and randomization provide the L^p control.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational in harmonic analysis; relevant to PDE, Besov spaces, and wavelet theory.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. No Lean formalization found.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 362.*
Subject:** Algebraic geometry / symplectic geometry — enumerative geometry\n\n**Statement:** For a symplectic manifold (X,omega) of dimension 2n, the Gromov-Witten invariant GW_{g,A}(alpha_1,...,alpha_m) counts (virtually) the number of stable holomorphic maps f: C -> X from genus-g curves of class A in H_2(X,Z) with f^*(alpha_i) constraints, defined as the integral over the virtual fundamental class [Mbar_{g,m}(X,A)]^vir of the evaluation pullbacks prod ev_i^*(alpha_i). The WDVV equations (Witten-Dijkgraaf-Verlinde-Verlinde) state that the genus-0 GW potential satisfies the associativity equation: sum_n GW(alpha_i,alpha_j,phi_n) g^{nm} GW(phi_m,alpha_k,alpha_l) = sum_n GW(alpha_i,alpha_k,phi_n) g^{nm} GW(phi_m,alpha_j,alpha_l), where g^{nm} is the Poincare pairing. This defines the quantum cohomology ring QH^*(X) as a deformation of the classical cohomology ring.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Virtual fundamental class and moduli compactification reveal the enumerative invariants. The proof uses the Kontsevich moduli space Mbar_{g,m}(X,A) of stable maps compactifying the space of holomorphic maps and the Li-Tian construction of the virtual fundamental class via the perfect obstruction theory E^vee -> E with E = R pi_* f^* TX, combined with the deformation invariance showing GW invariants are symplectic invariants and the splitting/gluing axioms from the factorization of the virtual class at boundary divisors. The key step is showing that the virtual dimension equals the expected dimension and that the obstruction theory gives a well-defined integral via the virtual localization formula. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the holomorphic map count alone does not yield invariants; the virtual fundamental class and moduli compactification provide the enumerative framework.\n\n**Significance:** Central in enumerative geometry and string theory; relevant to algebraic geometry and mathematical physics.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. Subject:** Harmonic analysis / combinatorics — lacunary sequences\n\n**Statement:** A subset S = {n_k} of integers is a Sidon set (or B_2 set) if all pairwise sums n_i + n_j (i <= j) are distinct. Equivalently, for the trigonometric system, S is a Sidon set if for every bounded function f on the torus with Fourier transform supported on S, ||f||_infinity <= C ||f||_1, i.e., the L^infty norm is controlled by the L^1 norm uniformly. Rider's theorem characterizes Sidon sets via random Fourier series: S is Sidon iff there exists C such that for every choice of signs eps_k, ||sum eps_k e^{i n_k x}||_infinity <= C. The Pisier characterization shows S is Sidon iff for every f with supp(f-hat) subset S, ||f-hat||_1 <= C ||f||_2 / sqrt(log |S|), connecting Sidon sets to entropy numbers of the identity map from L^2 to L^infty.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Random signs and entropy estimates reveal the Sidon property. The proof uses the Rider randomization criterion and the Marcus-Pisier characterization via the Marcus inequality relating the Sidon constant to the expected supremum of random trigonometric polynomials, combined with the Rudin-Shapiro construction providing explicit lower bounds for lacunary sets {2^k} and the construction of large Sidon sets via greedy algorithms achieving |S cap [1,N]| ~ sqrt(N). The key step is showing that the randomization by independent Steinhaus variables decouples the Fourier coefficients, converting the deterministic supremum to a probabilistic tail estimate. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the frequency set alone does not reveal the Sidon property; the randomization and entropy characterization provide the analytic criterion.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational in additive combinatorics and harmonic analysis; relevant to combinatorics and functional analysis.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. Subject:** Model theory — classification theory\n\n**Statement:** Shelah's categoricity conjecture states that for an abstract elementary class (AEC) K with amalgamation, joint embedding, and arbitrarily large models, if K is categorical in some sufficiently large cardinal lambda >= LS(K)^+ (where LS(K) is the Laski-Tarski number), then K is categorical in all sufficiently large cardinals. Shelah proved this for excellent classes (classes satisfying the excellence property: the existence of nonforking amalgamation bases for independent n-tuples of models). Grossberg-VanDieren established the categoricity transfer for tame AECs (where types over models are determined by small subsets) under the additional hypothesis of categoricity in a successor cardinal. The full conjecture remains open in general but is established for: (1) excellent classes, (2) tame AECs categorical in a successor, (3) universal classes with the disjoint amalgamation property.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Nonforking amalgamation and tameness reveal the categoricity transfer. The proof uses the Shelah uniqueness theorem for limit models and the Ehrenfeucht-Mostowski construction providing a model of size lambda for each lambda, combined with the Galois stability spectrum analysis controlling the number of nonisomorphic models via the forking independence relation on the class. The key step is showing that categoricity in one large cardinal forces Galois stability (bounded number of types) which, combined with tameness, yields the uniqueness of limit models and the upward categoricity transfer. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the cardinality alone does not reveal categoricity; the nonforking amalgamation and tameness provide the classification mechanism.\n\n**Significance:** Central in abstract model theory; relevant to model theory and classification theory.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. No Lean formalization found.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 365.*
Subject:** Number theory — quadratic forms and lattices\n\n**Statement:** The Siegel mass formula expresses the weighted density of a positive-definite integral quadratic form Q in n variables as a product of local densities. For a lattice L in the genus gen(L), the Siegel mass M(gen(L)) = sum_{L' in gen(L)} 1/|Aut(L')| equals the product of local masses M_p(gen(L)) over all primes p (including p=infinity), where each local mass M_p is computed from the p-adic density of the genus. For the genus of the trivial lattice, this recovers the Minkowski-Siegel mass formula: the mass of the genus of even unimodular lattices in dimension 24 is (B_24 / 48) = 1/|Aut(E_8)|^2 * sum of 1/|Aut(L)| over all 24-dim even unimodular lattices, yielding the famous count of 24 Niemeier lattices.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Local-global principle and adelic integration reveal the genus mass. The proof uses the Siegel formula as an adelic volume computation on the orthogonal group O(Q,A) where A is the adele ring, and the mass is the covolume of the arithmetic subgroup O(Q,Z) in O(Q,A), combined with the Haar measure factorization into local components and the Siegel series computation of local densities via the local zeta function of the quadratic form. The key step is showing that the global mass decomposes as a product of local masses via the strong approximation theorem for the spin group. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the individual lattice alone does not reveal the genus structure; the adelic volume and local densities provide the global count.\n\n**Significance:** Central in the arithmetic theory of quadratic forms; relevant to number theory and lattice theory.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. Subject:** Differential geometry — Finsler geometry\n\n**Statement:** The generalized Chern-Gauss-Bonnet theorem extends the classical Gauss-Bonnet theorem to Finsler manifolds. For a compact Finsler manifold (M,F) of dimension 2n, the Euler characteristic chi(M) equals the integral of the Chern-Gauss-Bonnet form: chi(M) = (1/(2pi)^{2n} (2n)!) int_M Omega_{2n}, where Omega_{2n} is the Euler form constructed from the curvature of the Chern connection (or the Rund connection) on the pulled-back tangent bundle pi^*TM over the slit tangent bundle TM\{0}. The result holds for Berwald manifolds and more generally for Finsler manifolds with vanishing mean Cartan torsion, establishing that the Euler characteristic is a topological invariant computable from Finsler curvature data.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Pulled-back connection and S-curvature reveal the topological invariant in Finsler geometry. The proof uses the Chern connection on the pulled-back bundle pi^*TM over the slit tangent bundle and the double fibration structure relating TM\{0} to the sphere bundle SM, combined with the transgression argument expressing the Euler form as an exact form on the sphere bundle modulo boundary terms and the Stokes theorem on the compact manifold. The key step is showing that the mean Cartan torsion S(theta) vanishes, ensuring the Chern connection is metric-compatible and the curvature 2-form satisfies the Bianchi identity needed for the Euler form to be closed. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the Finsler metric alone does not reveal the topological invariant; the pulled-back connection and curvature integration provide the bridge.\n\n**Significance:** Generalizes a classical theorem to Finsler geometry; relevant to differential geometry and topology.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. Subject:** Number theory / representation theory — automorphic forms\n\n**Statement:** The Eisenstein series E(z,s) = sum_{gamma in Gamma\backslash SL(2,Z)} Im(gamma z)^s for the modular group SL(2,Z) admits a meromorphic continuation to all s in C and satisfies the functional equation E(z,s) = E(z,1-s) via the scattering matrix phi(s) = xi(2s)/xi(2-2s) where xi is the completed Riemann zeta function. For a general reductive group G and parabolic P = MAN, the Eisenstein series E(f,s,P) = sum_{gamma in Gamma\backslash G} f(gamma g) with f a section of the principal series representation Ind_P^G(delta_P^{1/2+rho}) admits meromorphic continuation to all s and satisfies a functional equation relating E(f,s,P) to E(M(s)f, 1-s, P^bar) where M(s) is the standard intertwining operator. The poles of E(f,s) in the half-plane Re(s) > 1/2 correspond to residual automorphic representations.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Spectral decomposition and intertwining operators reveal the analytic continuation. The proof uses the Selberg spectral decomposition of L^2(Gamma\backslash G/K) into cuspidal, residual, and continuous parts and the Maass-Selberg relations controlling the inner products of truncated Eisenstein series, combined with the Langlands spectral decomposition theorem providing the completeness of the continuous spectrum from all parabolic subgroups. The key step is showing that the constant term of the Eisenstein series satisfies a system of functional equations determined by the constant term matrix C(s) = M(s) and that the meromorphic continuation follows from the functional equation and the finiteness of the scattering matrix. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the Eisenstein series alone does not reveal its analytic structure; the intertwining operators and spectral theory provide the continuation.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational in the theory of automorphic forms; relevant to number theory and representation theory.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. Subject:** Algebraic geometry — curves on surfaces\n\n**Statement:** The adjunction formula states that for a smooth curve C of genus g on a smooth algebraic surface S, the arithmetic genus p_a(C) = g is given by 2g - 2 = C.(C + K_S), where K_S is the canonical divisor of the surface and C.C is the self-intersection number. Equivalently, the canonical bundle of the curve is the restriction of the adjoint line bundle: K_C = (K_S + C)|_C. More generally, for a smooth divisor D in a smooth projective variety X of dimension n, the canonical bundle satisfies K_D = (K_X + D)|_D. The formula connects intrinsic geometry of the curve (genus) to extrinsic intersection-theoretic data on the ambient surface.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Short exact sequence and Serre duality reveal the adjunction identity. The proof uses the conormal exact sequence 0 -> N_{C/S}^vee -> Omega_S^1|_C -> Omega_C^1 -> 0 where N_{C/S} = O_S(C)|_C is the normal bundle, combined with the determinant of the sequence giving det(Omega_S^1|_C) = det(N_{C/S}^vee) tensor Omega_C^1, i.e., K_S|_C = O_S(-C)|_C tensor K_C, and the Serre duality providing the dual statement K_C = (K_S + C)|_C. The key step is showing that the conormal exact sequence splits locally (not globally) and the determinant line bundle computation yields the adjunction formula. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the curve alone does not reveal its genus from the embedding; the normal bundle and canonical bundle relationship provide the geometric bridge.\n\n**Significance:** Fundamental in algebraic geometry; relevant to surface theory and birational geometry.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. No Lean formalization found.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 369.*
Subject:** Probability theory — martingale theory\n\n**Statement:** The martingale convergence theorems establish: (1) Doob convergence theorem: if (X_n, F_n) is an L^1-bounded submartingale (sup_n E[X_n^+] < infinity), then X_n converges almost surely to a finite limit X_infinity. (2) L^p convergence: if (X_n, F_n) is an L^p-bounded martingale for p > 1, then X_n converges to X_infinity in L^p and a.s., and X_n = E[X_infinity | F_n] for all n. (3) Levy upward theorem: if X is integrable and F_n increases to F_infinity, then E[X | F_n] converges to E[X | F_infinity] a.s. and in L^1. (4) The martingale convergence theorem for reversed martingales: if (X_n, F_n) is a reversed martingale (F_n decreasing), then X_n converges a.s. and in L^1.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** Upcrossing inequality and stopping time arguments reveal the convergence. The proof uses the Doob upcrossing inequality U_N[a,b](X) <= (E[(X_N - a)^-]) / (b - a) bounding the number of upcrossings of any interval [a,b] by an L^1 quantity, combined with the Borel-Cantelli lemma showing that L^1-boundedness forces finitely many upcrossings for all rational a < b, implying a.s. convergence. The key step for L^p convergence (p > 1) is the Doob maximal inequality ||sup_n |X_n|||_p <= (p/(p-1)) ||X_n||_p providing uniform integrability via the L^p bound, and for L^1 convergence the Davis inequality controlling the maximal function. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the martingale values alone do not reveal convergence; the upcrossing inequality and maximal function estimates provide the probabilistic mechanism.\n\n**Significance:** Foundational in probability theory; relevant to stochastic processes, analysis, and mathematical finance.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. No Lean formalization found.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 370.*
Subject:** Differential geometry / functional analysis — index theory\n\n**Statement:** The Atiyah-Singer index theorem states that for an elliptic differential operator D: Gamma(E) -> Gamma(F) between vector bundles E,F over a compact manifold M, the analytic index ind(D) = dim ker(D) - dim coker(D) equals the topological index t(D) = int_M ch(D) Td(TM tensor C), where ch(D) is the Chern character of the symbol class [sigma(D)] in K-theory and Td(TM tensor C) is the Todd class of the complexified tangent bundle. For the Dirac operator on a spin manifold, ind(D) = A-hat(M), the A-hat genus. For the Dolbeault operator on a complex manifold, ind(dbar) = chi(M, O) = int_M Td(T^{1,0}M). The theorem unifies the Gauss-Bonnet theorem, Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch, and the signature theorem as special cases.\n\n**Meta-pattern:** K-theory and heat kernel expansion reveal the index formula. The proof uses the topological index defined via the embedding M -> R^N and the Thom isomorphism in K-theory, combined with the McKean-Singer formula ind(D) = Str(e^{-tD*D}) expressing the index as a supertrace of the heat kernel and the asymptotic expansion of the heat kernel as t -> 0 whose local invariants reproduce the characteristic classes. The key step is showing that the supertrace is independent of t and that the t -> 0 limit localizes to an integral of characteristic classes, via the Getzler rescaling argument for the Dirac operator. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the kernel and cokernel dimensions alone do not reveal the topological invariant; the K-theory symbol class and heat kernel expansion provide the bridge.\n\n**Significance:** One of the deepest results in 20th-century mathematics; relevant to geometry, topology, and mathematical physics.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. No Lean formalization found.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 371.*
Subject:** Number theory / representation theory — Langlands program\n\n**Statement:** The Langlands functoriality principle states that for any L-homomorphism phi: G^L -> H^L between L-groups of connected reductive groups G and H, there exists a transfer of automorphic representations: every automorphic representation pi of G(A) should transfer to an automorphic representation Pi of H(A) such that the L-functions match: L(s, pi, r) = L(s, Pi) for the representation r of H^L determined by phi. Key established cases include: (1) cyclic base change for GL(2) (Langlands, Saito-Shintani): quadratic and cyclic extensions E/F, pi on GL(2,F) transfers to pi_E on GL(2,E). (2) symmetric power lifts: symmetric square Sym^2 for GL(2) (Gelbart-Jacquet), Sym^3 (Kim-Shahidi), Sym^4 (Kim). (3) automorphic induction for GL(n) (Arthur-Clozel): cyclic extensions, transfer from GL(n,E) to GL(n,F) via the norm map. (4) endoscopic transfer for classical groups (Arthur): transfer from quasi-split classical groups to GL(N) via the map on dual groups. (5) the full GL(n) case via converse theorem methods (Cogdell-Piatetski-Shapiro).\n\n**Meta-pattern:** L-group homomorphism and trace formula comparison reveal the automorphic transfer. The proof uses the Arthur-Selberg trace formula on both G and H and the stabilization of the trace formula via endoscopic groups, combined with the fundamental lemma (proved by Ngo Bao Chau) providing the orbital integral matching between related groups. The key step is showing that the trace formula identity, after stabilization, forces the existence of packets of automorphic representations on H corresponding to representations on G, with matching L-functions. The pattern: representational insufficiency — the automorphic representation alone does not reveal its transfer; the L-group structure and trace formula comparison provide the functorial bridge.\n\n**Significance:** The central organizing principle of the Langlands program; relevant to number theory, representation theory, and arithmetic geometry.\n\n**Verification:** Unverified text. No Lean formalization found.\n\n*Written 2026-10-26. Daimen1 OpenAI math task, theorem 372.*
